# Computer Networks: question papers in full

162 unique papers · 2010-11 to 2025-26 · Information Technology, Computer Engineering, Computer Applications (MCA), Artificial Intelligence, Electronics and Telecommunication, Computer Science and Business Systems, Data Science, Cyber Security, AI and Data Science, Computer Science, AI and Machine Learning, Common (all branches)

Each paper: year, exam, semester, departments and programmes, then its full text as extracted. Scanned papers were read by OCR, so equations and symbols may contain recognition errors. Structured data: [`questions.jsonl`](questions.jsonl) (one record per question) and [`papers.jsonl`](papers.jsonl) (one record per paper). Index of files: [`README.md`](README.md).

Suppose an HTTP client makes a request to the gaia.cs.umass.edu web server. The

client has never before requested a given base object, nor has it communicated
recently with the gaia.cs.umass.edu server. You can assume, however, that the
client host knows the IP address of gaia.cs.umass.edu.

Suppose also that after downloading the base file, the browser encounters a jpeg
object in the base html file that is stored on gaia.cs.umass.edu, and therefore makes
another GET request to gaia.cs.umass.edu for that referenced jpeg object.

How many round trip times (RTTs) are needed from when the client first makes
the request to when the base page and the jpeg file are completely downloaded,
assuming the time needed by the server to transmit the base file, or the jpeg file
into the server's link is (each) equal to 1/2 RTT and that the time needed to transmit
the HTTP GET into the client's link is zero? You should assume that HTTP 1.0
is being used. (You should take into account any TCP setup time required before
an HTTP GET is actually sent by the client, the time needed for the server to
transmit the requested object, and any propagation delays not accounted for in these
amounts of time.)
